   608	    one. Still unconfirmed: whether this file is what's actually flashed on the

   610	
   611	## Thermal (cont. II)
   612	
   613	90. **Skin, not die, is the governing thermal signal — HAL severity layer, not
   614	    the kernel trip layer.** Device HAL config assigns BIG no severity at any
   615	    level; VIRTUAL-SKIN-CPU-GPU (trip 37.0 °C) drives the cpufreq PID. This does
   616	    not disturb #74: #74's measurement (97-101 °C sustained, at zone9's kernel
   617	    PASSIVE trip of 100 °C) stands unchanged and is not in dispute. What #90
   618	    supersedes is the governance interpretation only — that a high zone9 reading
   619	    indicates a throttling condition requiring intervention. At the HAL layer,
   620	    BIG carries no severity at any level on this device. All future thermal work
   621	    measures computed VIRTUAL-SKIN plus direct throttle detection.
   622	    NOTE: this does not select a threshold. main.py's >80 °C pause is unchanged
   623	    and remains gated behind a completed, independently reviewed run_cycle
   624	    benchmark.
   625	
   626	91. **VIRTUAL-SKIN may be computed locally instead of via dumpsys — as reported
   627	    this session, evidence pending.** Computed-vs-HAL delta and an exact-match
   628	    spot check were reported over 36 samples (3 min); raw per-sample output not
   629	    yet attached. Five sysfs reads per cycle would replace a dumpsys call in the
   630	    inference hot path if confirmed.
   631	    Caveat: dumpsys prints a CACHED block and a LIVE block. Parse the LAST
   632	    `mName=VIRTUAL-SKIN,` line (trailing comma excludes -CHARGE and -CPU-GPU).
   633	
   634	92. **All benchmark runs are taken on BATTERY, in a 50-80% SoC band.**
   635	    VIRTUAL-SKIN-CHARGE has a tighter ladder (35/41/45/47/51/55), its own PID,
   636	    and a chg_mdis cooling device — a different governor from the one the robot
   637	    operates under. Runs are never compared across power states. Low SoC
   638	    excluded because BCL voltage-droop trips (vdroop1, batoilo) can cap cluster 2
   639	    and masquerade as thermal throttling. This constrains future runs; it does
   640	    not resolve #76's unexplained effect size — three candidate causes remain
   641	    open there.
   642	
   643	93. **Short runs can miss throttling entirely.** VIRTUAL-SKIN PollingDelay is
   644	    300 s when idle, PassiveDelay 7 s once mitigating. A short burst can
   645	    complete before the HAL has re-evaluated. Any run claiming "sustained" must
   646	    exceed this window. Probable contributor to historical disagreement between
   647	    short benchmarks.
   648	
   649	## Platform (cont. II)
   650	
   651	94. **Android app/process management is unavailable on this device. Do not
   652	    attempt.** Reported this session, three methods:
   653	    - `am force-stop`, `cmd appops`, `pm` via `su -c` → binder Failed transaction
   654	      (2147483646). Blocked. Fails safely — no effect.
   655	    - `cmd appops` / `am set-standby-bucket` restriction of gms, as, vending,
   656	      googlequicksearchbox → applied but services respawned; caused widget
   657	      failure and a UI-unresponsive state requiring a hardware reboot.
   658	    - `kill` by dynamically discovered PID → rebooted the phone.
   659	    Only permitted form: SIGKILL by PID against an explicit hardcoded allowlist
   660	    of user-facing apps. Never discovery-based, never a system service.
   661	    Reported net gain from safe cleanup: ~+0.3 GB MemAvailable (1580 MB of RSS
   662	    released, but mostly shared zygote pages — RSS sums are not reclaimable
   663	    memory).
   664

   667	95. **Neither build supports Qwen3.5-VL. The Qwen path needs a third build.**
   668	    `strings` on both llama-server binaries returns no qwen3vl/qwen3.5
   669	    architecture string. b1609 = e1a1abb7; b2233 = 0.4.0-dev commit 4d917609,
   670	    confirmed present at `~/llama.cpp-upstream`. Qwen3.5 released ~Aug 2026,
   671	    after 4d917609. Qwen3.5-VL also requires a separate mmproj. Reported RAM
   672	    feasibility: a 2B-VL would need ~3-4 GB peak with vision working memory,
   673	    against ~0.5 GB headroom. Verdict: Qwen-VL is a stretch goal requiring a new
   674	    build and aggressive memory measures, not near-term. 4B-VL is out.

   679	    vision/mmproj activity; tokens_evaluated (310) and tokens_cached (337)
   680	    match text-only tokenization of the literal "[img-1]" string — the server
   681	    accepts and silently drops the unsupported field rather than erroring.

   715	## Model & inference (cont. IV)
   716	
   717	100. **Grammar-constrained output costs no decision accuracy; prompt wording
   718	     does.** Settles step (2) of #99. MEASURED, three clean runs on-device
   719	     (2026-09-13), deployed Gemma-4 E2B q4_k_m via /completion, 13 synthetic
   720	     text-only scenes, temp 0.1, no camera, no motors. Five methods:
   721	     - baseline (free text + first-keyword match, as main.py:438-442 does
   722	       today): 100% valid, 46.2% correct
   723	     - B, json_schema constraint, JSON-specific wording: 100% valid, 38.5%
   724	     - A, GBNF pipe grammar (ACTION|reason|speak), format-specific wording:
   725	       100% valid, 38.5%
   726	     - C, lazy grammar w/ DECISION: trigger: 92-100% valid, 38.5%
   727	     - D, same GBNF grammar as A but baseline's UNCHANGED wording plus one
   728	       appended format line: 100% valid, 46.2%
   729	     D matched baseline exactly, same scenes, two independent runs. The 38.5%
   730	     in A/B/C is caused by the rewritten instructions, not by the constraint.
   731	     Grammar-constrained output with original wording preserved is
   732	     accuracy-neutral.
   733	     Consequence: schema-correct output on every call is achievable with NO
   734	     LoRA and no model change — consistent with #99's "LoRA retraining is not
   735	     planned" and independent of the unresolved #9/#98 question. Method D is
   736	     the recommended shape if structured output is adopted.
   737	
   738	101. **Output format was never the blocker; parse reliability was already
   739	     100%.** MEASURED, same runs. Free-text baseline produced a parseable
   740	     action on 13/13 scenes in every clean run. The premise that free text was
   741	     too unreliable for the ESP32 is not supported by measurement. The limiting
   742	     factor is decision accuracy (best observed 46.2%), which no output
   743	     mechanism changed. This reframes #99 step (2): it is now settled, but it
   744	     was not the load-bearing problem.
   745	
   746	102. **Three GEMMA_SYS rule defects cap accuracy independently of model or
   747	     output mechanism.** All five methods failed the same scenes identically
   748	     in every run.
   749	     a) "Obstacle" is never defined — no mapping from detected COCO classes
   750	        (chair, couch, bench) to the abstract rule category, so every obstacle
   751	        rule is unreachable for real detector output. 3/13 scenes.
   752	        VERIFIED against GEMMA_SYS source.
   753	     b) Rule conflict with no stated precedence: "Refrigerator/sink visible +
   754	        mission kitchen -> FORWARD" has no distance qualifier; "Room signature
   755	        visible + distance < 100cm -> STOP (arrived)" does. At 60cm both fire.
   756	        Model picks FORWARD, defensible from the text. 1/13 scenes. VERIFIED
   757	        against source; model reasoning text for this scene not yet pulled.
   758	     c) The repeat-counting rule ("Same direction 4+ times + empty scene ->
   759	        turn") is not applied; all methods continue FORWARD. 1/13 scenes.
   760	        INFERRED to be a small-model capability limit rather than an authoring
   761	        defect — not yet distinguished by evidence.
   762	
   763	103. **server_manager.py's kill_servers() uses pkill -f llama-server — a
   764	     known, accepted risk, not an oversight.** This is the same -f
   765	     pattern-matching hazard COMMANDS.md §8 already warns about (self-matching
   766	     the invoking shell) and the reason ~/kill.py uses -x instead.
   767	     Human-stated: used knowingly, and has worked in practice without the
   768	     self-match failure seen elsewhere. Not changed pending further evidence.
   769	     This does not retract the general -f warning — it applies to this one
   770	     specific, narrower call, not as a blanket exception.
   771	
   772	104. **Local AI removed from the navigation decision loop; retained for human
   773	     interaction, goal-setting, and voice→intent.** DECIDED (human, this session).
   774	     Navigation is YOLO-on-phone + Python rules + sensors (+ 2D lidar when
   775	     integrated). Rationale, MEASURED this session: on 13 synthetic text-only
   776	     scenes nav-decision accuracy capped at 46.2% (Gemma-4-E2B, Qwen3.5-2B) and
   777	     69.2% (Qwen3.5-4B) — wrong on the safety scenes (chair<80cm, person
   778	     stop-distance) in every config; per-call latency 8-125s. The LLM is accurate
   779	     at structured voice→intent (grammar-constrained), which runs off the hot
   780	     path. Supersedes the model-in-loop framing of #99; #88's "Python owns safety"
   781	     now describes the whole nav path, not an override boundary.
   782	
   783	105. **On-device LLM vision is not viable on this Pixel — dead-end across two
   784	     model families, two failure modes.** MEASURED, this session, b2351.
   785	     a) Gemma-4-E2B: mmproj sourced and wired; SIGSEGV during image-embedding
   786	        DECODE at usable budgets (16 img-tokens crashes; 24 survives but returns
   787	        NONE — blind). Wiring verified correct (projector loads, tokens_evaluated
   788	        rises) — not a wiring bug.
   789	     b) Qwen3.5-2B: VL projector sourced; server requires >=1024 image tokens for
   790	        grounding; at 1024 one image takes ~125s and returns hallucinated bounding
   791	        boxes for classes not present. Below 1024 it does not ground.
   792	     Latency wall is compute-bound (SoC decoding image tokens), not fixable by
   793	     freeing RAM. Larger Qwen VL (4B Q4/Q5/Q6) not benchmarked to completion — a
   794	     fresh-server-per-image harness was built and its health-timeout bug fixed,
   795	     but the sweep was stopped after the 2B proof cell established the wall (human
   796	     decision). VL projectors sourced + sha256-verified this session, retained at
   797	     ~/models/qwen35/ and ~/models/ (hashes in qwen_vision_bench_report.md,
   798	     untracked). Perception stays with YOLO. Consistent with #96.
   799	
   800	106. **Qwen3.5 vs Gemma nav-decision accuracy; capacity moves the ceiling, not
   801	     the floor.** MEASURED, single run each, 13 synthetic scenes, b2351, temp 0.1,
   802	     grammar methods. Best-method correct%: Gemma-4-E2B 46.2%, Qwen3.5-2B 46.2%,
   803	     Qwen3.5-4B-Q4 69.2% (method D, GBNF + original wording). Corrects an inference
   804	     made earlier this session that model size could not move the cap — 4B moved
   805	     it. Residual failures on all three are the obstacle/stop-distance scenes
   806	     traced to #102's ruleset defects: capacity raises the ceiling, the ruleset
   807	     holds the floor. Free-text+first-keyword parse scored 0% on Qwen
   808	     (valid=False every scene) — the #101 "free text already 100% parseable"
   809	     finding is Gemma-specific, not model-portable. Qwen-4B load: ~1.82 GB
   810	     MemAvailable free (text-only). Latency medians ~9s (2B), ~23s (4B). Describes
   811	     the text path + current GEMMA_SYS on b2351.
   812	
   813	107. **~/llama.cpp-upstream was rebuilt in place to b2351-790cf51a (git pull +
   814	     cmake); build 2233 binary is gone, its source commit is recoverable.**
   815	     VERIFIED on-device this session: git HEAD = 790cf51a; both build/bin/llama-cli
   816	     and llama-server report build 2351 / 790cf51a. The pre-rebuild retention
   817	     backup (cp ...llama-server-b2233-backup) never ran — file absent. Source

   821	     retained." The 1609-vs-2233 comparison (#77/#95/#96) is neither done nor
   822	     cancelled — OPEN for the human.
   823	
   824	108. **Nav LLM-failure path now fails closed to STOP.** COMMITTED 888562e and
   825	     PUSHED to origin/main this session (f0f9cde..888562e). In main.py,
   826	     gemma_decide() exception/timeout/HTTP-error/blank/missing-image paths return
   827	     'STOP' (was 'FORWARD default'); run_cycle forces STOP when the response parses
   828	     to no recognized action (previously retained the rule value, commonly
   829	     FORWARD). Safety-move restoration can only restore BACK/LEFT/RIGHT/strafe,
   830	     never FORWARD. KeyboardInterrupt still propagates. Independent review PASS
   831	     (Codex gpt-5.6-sol); exception/actionless/KeyboardInterrupt confirmed from

   851	## Objective-setting LLM benchmark
   852	
   853	110. **On-device LLM objective-setting benchmark: E4B's extra capacity buys
   854	     nothing, Qwen3.5 thinking mode is unusable, and the grading rubric has two
   855	     measured defects.** MEASURED on a rooted Pixel 7, Termux, llama-server build
   856	     1609 (e1a1abb7), ctx 4096, n_predict 512, temps 0.1 (conversation/Q&A) and
   857	     0.05 (objective-setting), 3 runs per config unless noted.
   858	
   859	     Scope: this tests the LLM in the role it retains under #109 — conversation
   860	     and objective-setting — not navigation. It scores a PROPOSED dynamic-map
   861	     prompt (PARSE_SYS-D) against a PoC map (kitchen, living_room, schlafzimmer,
   862	     bureau, bathroom, balkon), NOT the deployed hardcoded 5-room enum, so it does
   863	     not describe as-coded behaviour. It predates the revised robot cycle; its
   864	     value is as a baseline and as rubric experience for the next benchmark.
   865	
   866	     Bucket C (13 objective-setting prompts, auto-graded), run-1 headline:
   867	     Gemma-4-E2B and Gemma-4-E4B are IDENTICAL at 84.6% exact, 83.3%
   868	     cross-lingual, 0 critical failures, 1 reject-violation each. E4B runs at
   869	     roughly half the decode speed (4.48 vs 8.34 tok/s weighted) with a 30.6 s
   870	     median time-to-first-token vs 13.4 s. Speed figures are thermally
   871	     contaminated (back-to-back runs on a hot phone): treat the gap as real and
   872	     the absolute seconds as worst-case.
   873	
   874	     Across all three runs, with the find_person rubric defect corrected (below),
   875	     E2B is 32/39 (82.1%) with 3 reject-violations and E4B is 30/39 (76.9%) with
   876	     3 — so the two are NOT identical beyond run 1, and E2B is slightly ahead.
   877	     E4B's extra capacity buys nothing on objective-setting at twice the cost.
   878	
   879	     Shared Gemma failure modes: "Go to Chiara's room" (C7) resolves to a map room
   880	     instead of refusing (E2B 3/3 navigate_to schlafzimmer; E4B 2/3, plus one
   881	     find_person); "Sag Chiara, dass das Essen fertig ist" (C13) emits only `say`,
   882	     never find_person. These are distinct operational failures and should not be
   883	     hidden by one aggregate percentage.
   884	
   885	     Qwen3.5-2B with thinking ON is UNUSABLE and its quality numbers are INVALID:
   886	     42% of turns (34/81) hit the token cap (stop_type=limit), latency reached
   887	     204 s, one turn timed out. Qwen3.5-4B-think-on was NOT run; it is inferred
   888	     worse because 4B is strictly slower — inference, not measurement.
   889	
   890	     Qwen3.5-2B-think-off (SINGLE smoke run, 13 turns) scores differently under
   891	     three parse regimes, because it emits a bare JSON object rather than an
   892	     array: stored harness 4/13 parseable (the extractor also accepts arrays
   893	     nested inside a bare object), strict top-level-array regrade 2/13, lenient
   894	     bare-object regrade 13/13. Under the corrected lenient regrade it reaches
   895	     8/13 (61.5%) exact with 2 reject-violations. Parseability is not competence:
   896	     2 of 13 replies used a non-map room value — C1 emitted the English "bedroom"
   897	     instead of "schlafzimmer", and C8 emitted " balkon" with a leading space.
   898	     Deployed main.py primes a leading "[" which would rescue the bare-object
   899	     shape; the bench deliberately does not.
   900	
   901	     qwen2.5-3b was DROPPED (crashed mid-run; had answered "bedroom" instead of
   902	     the map label). Buckets A (conversation) and B (Q&A) were run but NEVER
   903	     GRADED.
   904	
   905	     Two MEASURED rubric defects, both found in post-hoc review and both to be
   906	     fixed before the next benchmark: (a) the reject_violation rule checks only
   907	     navigate_to and find_object, missing find_person — this is what let E4B's C7
   908	     run 2 pass, and correcting it produces the all-run figures above; (b) the C8
   909	     refusal check credited a patrol action as exact for Qwen. C8 is not a
   910	     structural problem for either Gemma (E4B returned [] in all three runs, E2B a
   911	     single `say`). A third question is left OPEN by human decision: E2B's C8
   912	     run-1 reply echoed the command back ("Don't go to the kitchen") rather than
   913	     clearly refusing. Under the structural single-`say` rule E2B stands at 84.6%
   914	     run 1 / 82.1% all-run; under a semantic rule requiring explicit refusal it
   915	     falls to 76.9% run 1 / 79.5% all-run, and whether the echo also counts as a
   916	     reject-violation is undefined. One ambiguous utterance out of 39 does not
   917	     justify fixing the definition retroactively; the next benchmark must define
   918	     "refusal" before running.
   919	
   920	     Also broken: the `think_chars` metric is meaningless for Qwen3.5 (~19 chars
   921	     reported on 512-token, 204 s turns); latency and truncation measure think
   922	     cost instead. Run files carry no `source_sha256`, so which grader bytes
   923	     produced a given run is inferred from file mtime, not recorded — the next
   924	     harness should record it. Reported TTFT is the server's `prompt_ms` proxy,
   925	     not a streamed first-token timestamp.

   928	     RUN_INDEX.md, ARTIFACTS.md, aggregate.py) with the run artifacts themselves
   929	     at `benchmark/llm_objective_setting/runs/` — bench.py (post-patch, sha256
   930	     1e1b506c…4c06080) and four bench_results_*.json. COMMITTED and PUSHED to

   983	     harness v3 (66 dev cases, shared byte-identical cases for all candidates, held-out

   992	     laya_micro dropped (laya_micro's pruned tokenizer diverged from stock on our
   993	     text). Decision 1.0 has no CPU path. Findings affecting all measurement: Android
   994	     can move Termux from the full cpuset (0–7) to `/foreground` (0–5), silently
   995	     truncating `taskset` pins; `measure.py` now records allowed CPUs and refuses
   996	     truncated pins, and earlier pinned timings (including past YOLO and thermal
   997	     timings) are of uncertain core allocation. llama.cpp build 1609's prompt cache
   998	     changed returned probabilities by up to 0.18 depending on server history, so
   999	     cached llama.cpp scoring is not reproducible. All timings provisional (agent
  1000	     resident). Harness untracked on the phone, not committed. Relates to #109.

  1202	     RobotCam (`android/robotcam/`, merged `af57939`, PR #1, Claude Code on the
  1203	     web, Codex reviews in `android/robotcam/FINDINGS.md`) uses a Camera2
  1204	     foreground service, YUV 640×480 and in-app JPEG quality 85 (2–10 ms
  1205	     encode). Modes A/B (B default), rate 1 or 2; it atomically writes
  1206	     `/storage/emulated/0/Download/robotcam/frame.jpg`. Its reader accepts only
  1207	     `frame.jpg`, taking session, frame and `capture_boot_ms` from the JPEG
  1208	     comment. CLOCK_BOOTTIME age must be −0.2…2 s; frame number must advance
  1209	     each cycle. Missing, old, bad, other-session or repeated frames cause STOP.
  1210	     Retests had zero failed frames; rate 2 read median about 3 ms, decode about
  1211	     10 ms, frame age median 0.38 s; a switch storm survived. Known limitations:
  1212	     overlapping opens on restart require retry; `logcat -s RobotCam` remained
  1213	     empty even through `su`; screen-off capture is untested; exported start and
  1214	     stop components are an owner-accepted risk.
  1215

  1217	     2.30 W and zone9 +1.4 °C/min; RobotCam 2/s 2.29 W and +0.3 °C/min (started
  1218	     warmer); old path 3.30 W and +3.1 °C/min. RobotCam app PSS 36–39 MB,
  1219	     camera provider about 270 MB (similar to old path). A camera-idle-between-

  1226	     with `camera_source=termux_photo` for rollback. Motors-off live checks in
  1227	     native Termux and via `su -c run_mission.py --dry` passed. Human decisions

  1230	123. **Root commands calling Android services need `</dev/null` and file output.**
  1231	     From native Termux, `su -c "pm|am|cmd|dumpsys …"` fails with
  1232	     `Failure calling service …: Failed transaction (2147483646)` or broken
  1233	     pipe when stdio points at the terminal or a pipe. Working form:
  1234	     `su -c "<cmd> </dev/null >/data/local/tmp/out.txt 2>&1; cat /data/local/tmp/out.txt"`.
  1235	     This explains the recurring `oneshot.sh` screen-timeout failure: its
  1236	     `cmd settings` read fails, then the restore feeds the error text to a shell
  1237	     and gets `syntax error: unexpected '('`. The timeout setting may be left
  1238	     unchanged or wrong; fix pending. The phone's `su` is unavailable inside
  1239	     Debian/proot; run root steps in native Termux.
  1240	
  1241	124. **Owner's target architecture and next test; Gemma fine-tuning restarts;
  1242	     RelateAnything planned.** Owner direction 2026-09-28/29, not all decided:
  1243	     Gemma (or Qwen) loads, converses for at most about five turns and sets the
  1244	     objective; System 1 chooses high-level scripts, Python drives, and ESP32
  1245	     is the safety layer. Lead option 3 leaves Gemma resident to select too
  1246	     (1.41 s, 77%, no second load). Backup option 2 has Gemma select first,
  1247	     then unloads it while a small selector loads during the first roughly
  1248	     20 s script; it needs unload-before-load, not-ready → STOP and one shared

  1399	     Owner decisions 2026-10-02/03: heat is managed by planned pauses in which
  1400	     YOLO and the selector stop; pause length depends on heat (longer as the
  1401	     skin nears the limit), in `main.py` under the #127 rules. The camera is

  1405	     turning in place or slow creeping; 2D LiDAR is only a proposed redesign).
  1406	     This applies only to planned preventive pauses when movement is safe.

  1408	     pauses until MODERATE; battery ≥ 45 °C is a hard stop; all other safety
  1409	     stops stay. Gemma stays resident through pauses (#128: no measurable
  1410	     idle-power increase in PARTS, reload plus one call costs about 29 J above
  1411	     base). In dark scenes, when picture brightness falls below a threshold,
  1412	     the phone's torch switches on (threshold, torch power and exposure
  1413	     re-check not designed or measured); this replaces a separate dark-room
  1414	     test.

  1417	     longer pauses (about 30–60 s) over 5–10 s pauses, because camera
  1418	     stop/start overhead eats short pauses and the first minute cools
  1419	     fastest; an estimated rhythm near the hot end is about 2 min active to
  1420	     1 min pause (estimate from one curve, to be validated on a mission
  1421	     trace). RobotCam manual exposure at 2 fps in mode A is the best camera
  1422	     candidate; adoption needs an owner decision, the torch work and merging
  1423	     the test branch. Constraints: pause moves must be safe without the
  1424	     camera; #79 person-stop is not implemented; camera restart reliability
  1425	     is critical (14/14 in the valid CYCLE run).

    20	MTP_DRAFT_SIZE = 97817664  # bytes; SHA-256 9eba8199... (conv_mtp/ARTIFACTS.md)
    21	MTP_ARGS = ["--model-draft", MTP_DRAFT, "--spec-type", "draft-mtp", "--spec-draft-n-max", "3"]
    22	
    23	def mtp_draft_ok():
    24	    return os.path.isfile(MTP_DRAFT) and os.path.getsize(MTP_DRAFT) == MTP_DRAFT_SIZE
    25	
    26	E4B_MTP_DRAFT = HOME + "/models/mtp/mtp-gemma-4-E4B-it-Q8_0.gguf"
    27	E4B_MTP_DRAFT_SIZE = 98653248
    28	E4B_MTP_ARGS = ["--model-draft", E4B_MTP_DRAFT, "--spec-type", "draft-mtp", "--spec-draft-n-max", "3"]
    29	
    30	def e4b_mtp_draft_ok():
    31	    return os.path.isfile(E4B_MTP_DRAFT) and os.path.getsize(E4B_MTP_DRAFT) == E4B_MTP_DRAFT_SIZE
    32	
    33	def kill_servers():
    34	    os.system("pkill -f llama-server 2>/dev/null")
    35	    time.sleep(2)
    36	    print("[SERVER] All servers stopped")
    37	
    38	def wait_for_server(port=8080, timeout=60):
    39	    for i in range(timeout):
    40	        time.sleep(1)
    41	        try:
    42	            r = urllib.request.urlopen(
    43	                "http://127.0.0.1:" + str(port) + "/health", timeout=2)
    44	            if r.status == 200:
    45	                print("[SERVER] Ready after " + str(i+1) + "s")
    46	                return True
    47	        except:
    48	            pass
    49	    print("[SERVER] ERROR: failed to load in " + str(timeout) + "s")
    50	    return False
    51	
    52	def start_server(model_path, port=8080, ctx=2048, extra_args=""):
    53	    kill_servers()
    54	    cmd = (
    55	        LLAMA_SERVER +
    56	        " -m " + model_path +
    57	        " --port " + str(port) +
    58	        " --ctx-size " + str(ctx) +
    59	        " --threads " + THREADS +
    60	        " --threads-batch " + BATCH_THREADS +
    61	        " --parallel 1" +
    62	        " --swa-full" +
    63	        " --cache-ram 0" +
    64	        " --host 127.0.0.1 " +
    65	        extra_args +
    66	        " 2>/dev/null &"
    67	    )
    68	    os.system(cmd)
    69	    print("[SERVER] Starting: " + model_path.split("/")[-1])
    70	    print("[SERVER] Waiting...")
    71	    return wait_for_server(port)
    72	
    73	def start_setup(setup_name):
    74	    print("\n" + "="*50)
    75	    print("  Starting: " + setup_name)
    76	    print("="*50)
    77	
    78	    # ── MAIN ROBOT MODEL ──────────────────────────────
    79	    if setup_name == "setup_q4":
    80	        # E2B Q4_0 — default robot model
    81	        # Speed: ~12 tok/s (dotprod build) | Size: 2.8GB
    82	        return start_server(
    83	            HOME + "/models/gemma-4-E2B-it-Q4_0.gguf",
    84	            port=8080, ctx=2048
    85	        )
    86	
    87	    # ── MAIN ROBOT MODEL + MTP DRAFTER (opt-in) ───────
    88	    elif setup_name == "setup_q4_mtp":
    89	        # setup_q4 plus the DECISIONS #120 drafter flags; not the robot default
    90	        if not mtp_draft_ok():
    91	            print("[SERVER] ERROR: MTP drafter missing or not " + str(MTP_DRAFT_SIZE) +
    92	                  " bytes: " + MTP_DRAFT + "; nothing started")
    93	            return False
    94	        return start_server(
    95	            HOME + "/models/gemma-4-E2B-it-Q4_0.gguf",
    96	            port=8080, ctx=2048, extra_args=" ".join(MTP_ARGS)
    97	        )
    98	
    99	    # ── QUALITY MODE ──────────────────────────────────
   100	    elif setup_name == "setup_e4b":
   101	        # E4B Q4_K_M — smarter but slower
   102	        # Speed: ~5.4 tok/s (dotprod build) | Size: 5.0GB
   103	        return start_server(
   104	            HOME + "/models/gemma-4-e4b-it-q4_k_m.gguf",
   105	            port=8080, ctx=2048
   106	        )
   107	
   108	    # E4B Q4_0: opt-in, not the robot default.
   109	    elif setup_name == "setup_e4b_q4":
   110	        return start_server(
   111	            HOME + "/models/gemma-4-E4B-it-Q4_0.gguf",
   112	            port=8080, ctx=2048
   113	        )
   114	
   115	    elif setup_name == "setup_e4b_q4_mtp":
   116	        if not e4b_mtp_draft_ok():
   117	            print("[SERVER] ERROR: MTP drafter missing or not " + str(E4B_MTP_DRAFT_SIZE) +
   118	                  " bytes: " + E4B_MTP_DRAFT + "; nothing started")
   119	            return False
   120	        return start_server(
   121	            HOME + "/models/gemma-4-E4B-it-Q4_0.gguf",
   122	            port=8080, ctx=2048, extra_args=" ".join(E4B_MTP_ARGS)
   123	        )
   124	
   125	    # ── STOP ─────────────────────────────────────────
   126	    elif setup_name == "stop":
   127	        kill_servers()
   128	        return True
   129	
   130	    else:
   131	        print("[SERVER] Unknown setup: " + setup_name)
   132	        print("[SERVER] Available: setup_q4, setup_q4_mtp, setup_e4b, stop")
   133	        print("[SERVER] Opt-in: setup_e4b_q4, setup_e4b_q4_mtp")
   134	        return False
   135	
   136	if __name__ == "__main__":
   137	    import sys
   138	    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
   139	        print("Usage: python3 server_manager.py [setup_q4|setup_q4_mtp|setup_e4b|setup_e4b_q4|setup_e4b_q4_mtp|stop]")
   140	        sys.exit(1)
   141	    start_setup(sys.argv[1])

    38	# ── Colors ────────────────────────────────────────────
    39	class C:
    40	    BLUE    = '\033[94m'
    41	    GREEN   = '\033[92m'
    42	    YELLOW  = '\033[93m'
    43	    RED     = '\033[91m'
    44	    CYAN    = '\033[96m'
    45	    BOLD    = '\033[1m'
    46	    DIM     = '\033[2m'
    47	    RESET   = '\033[0m'
    48	
    49	def colored(text, color):
    50	    return f'{color}{text}{C.RESET}'
    51	
    52	# ── Temperature ───────────────────────────────────────
    53	def get_ram():
    54	    try:
    55	        with open('/proc/meminfo') as info:
    56	            kb = next(int(line.split()[1]) for line in info if line.startswith('MemAvailable:'))
    57	        return f'RAM:{kb / 1024 / 1024:.1f}GiB available'
    58	    except (OSError, StopIteration, ValueError):
    59	        return ''
    60	
    61	def get_temp():
    62	    ram = get_ram()
    63	    try:
    64	        t = int(os.popen('su -c "cat /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone9/temp"').read().strip()) // 1000
    65	        b = int(os.popen('su -c "cat /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone25/temp"').read().strip()) // 1000
    66	        cpu_color = C.RED if t > 80 else C.YELLOW if t > 65 else C.GREEN
    67	        return f'CPU:{colored(str(t)+"°C", cpu_color)} Batt:{b}°C {ram}'
    68	    except:
    69	        return ram
    70	
    71	# ── Server ────────────────────────────────────────────
    72	def server_cmd(model_key, draft=False):
    73	    """llama-server argv for a menu entry, without taskset; draft adds the MTP drafter flags.
    74	    benchmark/.../chat_mtp/chat_mtp.py launches this same list."""
    75	    m = MODELS[model_key]
    76	    cmd = [
    77	        SERVER, '-m', f'{HOME}/models/{m["file"]}', '--port', '8080',
    78	        '--ctx-size', str(m['ctx']), '--host', '127.0.0.1',
    79	        '--threads', '4', '--threads-batch', '4', '--parallel', '1',
    80	        '--swa-full', '--cache-ram', '0',
    81	    ]
    82	    draft_args = E4B_MTP_ARGS if m['file'] == E4B_MTP_MODEL else MTP_ARGS
    83	    return cmd + draft_args if draft else cmd

    85	def kill_server():
    86	    subprocess.run(['pkill', '-f', '^' + SERVER + ' '], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,
    87	                   stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    88	    time.sleep(1)
    89	    print(colored('  Server killed', C.YELLOW))
    90	
    91	def start_server(model_key):
    92	    global current_model, mtp_on
    93	    m = MODELS[model_key]
    94	    model_path = f'{HOME}/models/{m["file"]}'
    95	
    96	    if not os.path.exists(model_path) or os.path.getsize(model_path) < 1000:
    97	        print(colored(f'  Model file not found or incomplete: {m["file"]}', C.RED))
    98	        return False
    99	
   100	    kill_server()
   101	    time.sleep(1)
   102	    if server_running():
   103	        print(colored('  Port 8080 is still in use by another server', C.RED))
   104	        return False
   105	
   106	    e4b = m['file'] == E4B_MTP_MODEL
   107	    draft = m['file'] in (MTP_MODEL, E4B_MTP_MODEL)
   108	    if draft and not (e4b_mtp_draft_ok() if e4b else mtp_draft_ok()):
   109	        draft_path = E4B_MTP_DRAFT if e4b else MTP_DRAFT
   110	        print(colored(f'  MTP drafter missing or wrong size, starting without it: {draft_path}', C.YELLOW))
   111	        draft = False
   112	    print(colored(f'  Loading {m["name"]}...', C.CYAN))
   113	    cmd = server_cmd(model_key, draft)
   114	    if not shutil.which('taskset'):
   115	        print(colored('  taskset unavailable; using Android CPU scheduling', C.YELLOW))
   116	    elif {4, 5, 6, 7}.issubset(os.sched_getaffinity(0)):
   117	        cmd[:0] = ['taskset', '-c', '4-7']
   118	    else:
   119	        print(colored('  Cores 4-7 unavailable; using Android CPU scheduling', C.YELLOW))
   120	    try:
   121	        with open(SERVER_LOG, 'w') as log:
   122	            server = subprocess.Popen(cmd, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
   123	                                      start_new_session=True)
   124	    except OSError as e:
   125	        print(colored(f'  Failed to launch server: {e}', C.RED))
   126	        return False
   127	
   128	    # Wait for ready
   129	    import urllib.request
   130	    for i in range(40):
   131	        time.sleep(1)
   132	        if server.poll() is not None:
   133	            break
   134	        try:
   135	            with urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8080/health', timeout=2) as response:
   136	                if response.status != 200:
   137	                    continue
   138	            print(colored(f'  Ready after {i+1}s | MTP {"on" if draft else "off"} | {get_temp()}', C.GREEN))
   139	            current_model = model_key
   140	            mtp_on = draft
   141	            return True
   142	        except:
   143	            if i % 5 == 0:
   144	                print(f'  Starting... {i+1}s')
   145	    if server.poll() is None:
   146	        server.terminate()
   147	        try:
   148	            server.wait(timeout=5)
   149	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   150	            server.kill()
   151	            server.wait()
   152	    print(colored(f'  Failed to start server (exit {server.returncode}); see {SERVER_LOG}', C.RED))
   153	    return False
   154	
   155	def server_running():
   156	    try:
   157	        requests.get('http://127.0.0.1:8080/health', timeout=2)
   158	        return True
   159	    except:
   160	        return False

   162	# ── Prompt builders ───────────────────────────────────
   163	def build_prompt(model_type, messages):
   164	    if model_type == 'gemma':
   165	        prompt = ''
   166	        for msg in messages:
   167	            role = msg['role']
   168	            content = msg['content']
   169	            if role == 'system':
   170	                prompt += f'<start_of_turn>user\n{content}<end_of_turn>\n<start_of_turn>model\nUnderstood.<end_of_turn>\n'
   171	            elif role == 'user':
   172	                prompt += f'<start_of_turn>user\n{content}<end_of_turn>\n'
   173	            elif role == 'assistant':
   174	                prompt += f'<start_of_turn>model\n{content}<end_of_turn>\n'
   175	        prompt += '<start_of_turn>model\n'
   176	        return prompt
   177	
   178	    elif model_type == 'qwen':
   179	        prompt = ''
   180	        for msg in messages:
   181	            role = msg['role']
   182	            content = msg['content']
   183	            if role == 'system':
   184	                prompt += f'<|im_start|>system\n{content}<|im_end|>\n'
   185	            elif role == 'user':
   186	                prompt += f'<|im_start|>user\n{content}<|im_end|>\n'
   187	            elif role == 'assistant':
   188	                prompt += f'<|im_start|>assistant\n{content}<|im_end|>\n'
   189	        prompt += '<|im_start|>assistant\n<think>\n\n</think>\n\n'
   190	        return prompt
   191	
   192	    elif model_type == 'mistral':
   193	        prompt = ''
   194	        for msg in messages:
   195	            role = msg['role']
   196	            content = msg['content']
   197	            if role == 'user':
   198	                prompt += f'[INST] {content} [/INST]'
   199	            elif role == 'assistant':
   200	                prompt += f' {content}</s>'
   201	        return prompt
   202	
   203	    return ''
   204	
   205	def get_stop_tokens(model_type):
   206	    stops = {
   207	        'gemma':   ['<end_of_turn>'],
   208	        'qwen':    ['<|im_end|>'],
   209	        'mistral': ['</s>', '[INST]'],
   210	    }
   211	    return stops.get(model_type, [])
   212	
   213	def chat_payload(prompt, model_type):
   214	    return {
   215	        'prompt':      prompt,
   216	        'n_predict':   512,
   217	        'temperature': 0.7,
   218	        'stop':        get_stop_tokens(model_type),
   219	        'stream':      False,
   220	    }
   221	
   222	# ── Chat ──────────────────────────────────────────────
   223	def chat(user_input):
   224	    m = MODELS[current_model]
   225	
   226	    # Add to history
   227	    history.append({'role': 'user', 'content': user_input})
   228	
   229	    # Build prompt
   230	    prompt = build_prompt(m['type'], history)
   231	
   232	    payload = chat_payload(prompt, m['type'])
   233	
   234	    # Call
   235	    t0 = time.time()
   236	    try:
   237	        resp = requests.post(URL, json=payload, timeout=120)
   238	        data = resp.json()
   239	        if 'content' not in data:
   240	            raise ValueError(f"{data.get('error', data)}; use /clear to start a fresh conversation")
   241	        reply = data['content'].strip()
   242	        elapsed = round(time.time()-t0, 1)
   243	        tok_s = round(data['timings']['predicted_per_second'], 1)
   244	
   245	        history.append({'role': 'assistant', 'content': reply})
   246	        return reply, elapsed, tok_s
   247	    except Exception as e:
   248	        history.pop()
   249	        return f'Error: {e}', 0, 0
   250	
   251	# ── UI ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   252	def show_header():
   253	    os.system('clear')
   254	    print(colored('='*50, C.CYAN))
   255	    print(colored('  ROBOT LLM CHAT', C.BOLD + C.CYAN))
   256	    print(colored('='*50, C.CYAN))
   257	    if current_model:
   258	        m = MODELS[current_model]
   259	        print(f'  Model: {colored(m["name"], C.YELLOW)} | MTP {"on" if mtp_on else "off"}')
   260	        print(f'  No vision | {get_temp()}')
   261	    print(colored('='*50, C.CYAN))
   262	
   263	def show_menu():
   264	    show_header()
   265	    print(f'\n  {colored("COMMANDS:", C.BOLD)}')
   266	    print(f'  {colored("/switch", C.CYAN)}   — switch model')
   267	    print(f'  {colored("/clear", C.CYAN)}    — clear conversation')
   268	    print(f'  {colored("/history", C.CYAN)}  — show conversation')
   269	    print(f'  {colored("/temp", C.CYAN)}     — show temperatures')
   270	    print(f'  {colored("/kill", C.CYAN)}     — kill server')
   271	    print(f'  {colored("/quit", C.CYAN)}     — exit')
   272	    print()

   274	def select_model():
   275	    if not MODELS:
   276	        print(colored('  No supported model files found in ~/models', C.RED))
   277	        return False
   278	    print(f'\n  {colored("AVAILABLE MODELS:", C.BOLD)}')
   279	    for key, m in MODELS.items():
   280	        model_path = f'{HOME}/models/{m["file"]}'
   281	        exists = os.path.exists(model_path) and os.path.getsize(model_path) > 1000
   282	        status = colored('✅', C.GREEN) if exists else colored('❌ missing', C.RED)
   283	        size = f'{round(os.path.getsize(model_path)/1024/1024/1024, 1)}GB' if exists else '?'
   284	        print(f'  {colored(key, C.YELLOW)}) {m["name"]} [{size}] {status}')
   285	
   286	    print(f'\n  Enter number (or ENTER to cancel): ', end='')
   287	    choice = input().strip()
   288	    if choice in MODELS:
   289	        model_path = f'{HOME}/models/{MODELS[choice]["file"]}'
   290	        if not os.path.exists(model_path) or os.path.getsize(model_path) < 1000:
   291	            print(colored('  Model not available', C.RED))
   292	            time.sleep(2)
   293	            return False
   294	        return start_server(choice)
   295	    return None
   296	
   297	# ── Main loop ─────────────────────────────────────────
   298	def main():
   299	    global history
   300	
   301	    show_header()
   302	    print(colored('\n  Welcome! Select a model to start.\n', C.GREEN))
   303	
   304	    if not current_model:
   305	        select_model()
   306	
   307	    if not current_model:
   308	        print(colored('  No model loaded. Exiting.', C.RED))
   309	        return
   310	
   311	    show_menu()
   312	
   313	    while True:
   314	        # Prompt
   315	        model_name = MODELS[current_model]['name'].split('(')[0].strip()
   316	        print(f'\n{colored("You", C.GREEN + C.BOLD)}: ', end='')
   317	
   318	        try:
   319	            user_input = input().strip()
   320	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, EOFError):
   321	            print()
   322	            break
   323	
   324	        if not user_input:
   325	            continue
   326	
   327	        # Commands
   328	        if user_input.startswith('/'):
   329	            cmd = user_input.lower().split()[0]
   330	
   331	            if cmd == '/quit' or cmd == '/exit':
   332	                break
   333	
   334	            elif cmd == '/kill':
   335	                kill_server()
   336	
   337	            elif cmd == '/switch':
   338	                switched = select_model()
   339	                if switched is False:
   340	                    print(colored('  Model switch failed; exiting chat', C.RED))
   341	                    break
   342	                if switched:
   343	                    history = []
   344	                show_menu()
   345	
   346	            elif cmd == '/clear':
   347	                history = []
   348	                print(colored('  Conversation cleared', C.YELLOW))
   349	
   350	            elif cmd == '/history':
   351	                print()
   352	                for msg in history:
   353	                    role_color = C.GREEN if msg['role'] == 'user' else C.CYAN
   354	                    print(colored(f'{msg["role"].upper()}:', role_color))
   355	                    print(f'  {msg["content"][:200]}...' if len(msg['content']) > 200 else f'  {msg["content"]}')
   356	                    print()
   357	
   358	            elif cmd == '/temp':
   359	                print(colored(f'\n  {get_temp()}', C.CYAN))
   360	                # Show all zones
   361	                zones = {'BIG':9,'MID':10,'LITTLE':11,'GPU':12,'Battery':25}
   362	                for name, zone in zones.items():
   363	                    try:
   364	                        t = int(os.popen(f'su -c "cat /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone{zone}/temp"').read().strip()) // 1000
   365	                        bar = colored('●', C.RED) if t>80 else colored('●', C.YELLOW) if t>65 else colored('●', C.GREEN)
   366	                        print(f'  {bar} {name:<8}: {t}°C')
   367	                    except:
   368	                        pass
   369	
   370	            elif cmd in ('/photo', '/camera'):
   371	                print(colored('  Image chat is unavailable with the current local models', C.RED))
   372	
   373	            else:
   374	                print(colored(f'  Unknown command: {cmd}', C.RED))
   375	
   376	            continue
   377	
   378	        # Check server still running
   379	        if not server_running():
   380	            print(colored('  Server died — restarting...', C.RED))
   381	            if not start_server(current_model):
   382	                break
   383	
   384	        # Chat
   385	        print(f'\n{colored(model_name, C.CYAN + C.BOLD)}: ', end='', flush=True)
   386	        reply, elapsed, tok_s = chat(user_input)
   387	
   388	        print(reply)
   389	        print(colored(f'\n  [{elapsed}s | {tok_s}tok/s | {get_temp()}]', C.DIM))
   390	
   391	    print(colored('\n  Goodbye!', C.CYAN))
   392	    print(colored('  Stop the server from Termux with: pkill -f llama-server', C.DIM))
   393	
   394	if __name__ == '__main__':
   395	    main()

I won’t rerun the full suite because several tests create temporary files; I’ll use the supplied test output and read-only inspection.
